The levy does not happen at a first; it has been happening continuously, a twentieth of a day, since the ninth hour of the twenty-fourth of the month beginning 2,054. **And at not one first in nineteen years did anything arrive.**
- **A line does not come back.** A record belongs to whoever keeps it, and a man who has kept one for nine years has never once been able to take a line out of it. **There is no version of ch 717 in which she changes her mind on the day.**
- **What a line has to be: a date, a place, and true statements, and the third has to be not her.** A name is a person; a figure is not. **A board goes up on a first and there is not one other date it will travel on.**
- **THE BENCH.** The woman of sixty was on the board for four years and came off it and was told by nobody. Ning Yan has walked past that bench four times in nine years knowing why and has said nothing and has said out loud that she is not going to put it down. **The fourth name from the bottom of the fourth column has not been spoken and is not to be.**
- **THE SEVENTH FIRST went by at the frame with her at it for the second time in nine years**, the slate turned over, the figure under her name **one hundred and eighty-eight days**, right, and a man who does not know he is writing a levy writing four more lines of one. **She wrote nothing.**
- **The fourteen.** Her third reason for not telling them expired twenty-five days before the fact, because the arithmetic is about to be public property on a stick at a frame two days of road west. **She is not going to tell them, and it is not any of her three reasons.**
- **NING WU, at ch 713 and nowhere else in this batch.** She told him she has worked out her own total and he told her he worked his out five years ago and told nobody, and they said the same sentence to each other from opposite ends of a row of stones. **The year is not mentioned once and nothing is settled.**
- **The mountain, the last time.** A locked drawer thirty feet away with the key in her bag. **She went up eight flights, stood at the outside of a low door and did not go through it.** And an old man with a shut book went up the mountain road past her and did not stop and it was not to her. **He is not named and nobody was thanked.**
- **She worked out that the count she cannot escape is the one she wrote herself**, and she noticed something settling out of the ground under her feet and not out of her hands and **decided not to count it, which is the first thing in nineteen years she has decided not to count.**
- **IMMORTAL VESSEL 3 at ch 716, day 2,290**, and its cost named once, in another mouth: **she cannot be counted where she stands.** She never says it.
- **THE EIGHTH FIRST at ch 717, day 2,294, at the first hour, at the two posts, outdoors, on an open arm:** **nineteen words in her own hand with a date and a place on them — *Ning Yan, the two posts. Two hundred and eighteen days. One in twenty. Ten days, twenty-one hours, thirty-six minutes* — true, because her hand cannot write a false line, and nobody writes one twice. The little finger of her right hand stopped being part of her hand in the middle of it. Three fingers and a thumb. The arm did not close.**
- **ch 718, the batch's irreversible step: the designation is carried up on a board in her own hand instead of collected off her. The levy takes the writing and not the person. There is no instrument in the world that takes a line out of a book that has already gone up a stair.** **Nine seconds of sound at a frame in the first light of a morning, never printed in the book, and four accounts that do not agree.** **The bill for Court is settled.** She is not one of the four.
- **ch 717 does not close on her standing still.** The last thing on that page is her working out, standing in the grit in daylight, that the line is going into a room above her and that she will never know who read it or when, **and that the four of them at that turn are going to go on saying four different things about the same nine seconds for the rest of their lives and not one of them will ever be able to correct the other three.** That is what Volume 13 opens against.
- **ch 719 and 720, the stair and the threshold.** Eight flights and eight hundred and eighty steps on an arm that has been open since the morning she wrote the line, the third landing, the short breath, a bar across a door with the dust not broken. **Her decision at ch 719 is to leave the bag at the bottom of the first four and come up in the morning with an empty hand, and ch 720 pays it off: she climbs with the bar and nothing else, and she is carrying a thing and not bringing a gift.** **The low door is standing open at the seventh of the month beginning 2,294 and the bar is on the floor inside with the dust broken at one end and not at the other and nothing in the volume explains it.** A room with a long table in it, a lamp in a cage, and a second stair going up out of the far side. **She went up one step and stopped there.**

### What the batch resolved

**Volume 11's question, and it is answered: *whether a designation made to be harvested can be carried up instead.* It can.** It has to be carried by a piece of paper and not by a person, because a person cannot be counted where she stands and cannot keep what she finds by holding it, and because the only instrument in the world that takes a person through a wall is a thing seen truly and written truly. **Volume 12's own question, asked at ch 665 and given its answer at ch 698, is not re-asked and not re-answered.** The volume's goal is discharged: she went up, the designation went up ahead of her on a board in her own hand, and the levy came off the board instead of coming off her.

### What is still open, and what Volume 13 inherits

- **How far the rows sit past the wall. STILL OPEN AND STILL NOT SETTLED.** Two or three hundred yards at ch 618, four miles at ch 659 and 660, three miles at ch 677. **Batch 0004 puts no mile figure on the rows-to-turn leg in either direction, and the unit rule stands: thirteen miles is wall-to-turn, two days of road is rows-to-turn, two miles of flat is ground-to-wall past the gate, four miles is square-to-summit.**
- ***Twenty-two days*, unplaced.** `grep -oi 'twenty-two days'` over `chapters/volume-12/*.md` returns **0** and it must still return 0. **A Volume 13 card may put it in front of her again and may not equate it to a dated interval.**
- **The two thousand nine hundred and twenty**, the difference between ninety-eight thousand and ninety-five thousand and eighty on a sheet on a wall in a room with the shutters up. **Attributed to nothing in this volume and not to be.**
- **The four names in the fourth column, and one of them on a bench four hundred yards west of a frame.** She is not entitled to say a word of it and there is not one person in this county who can read that column. **Three hundred and one out of nine hundred and twelve is very nearly a third and she does not know what a third of a name is.**
- **A brother in a barrow ground who has counted four thousand stones a day for five years and gave the number to one person and said a thing about boards that she has not answered.**
- **Fourteen people in a ground of two hundred who can count to eighty, nine of them doing sums without her, forty who can count to eighty at all, and she has told none of them.**
- **A boy of nineteen who told her once, as arithmetic and not as a warning, that if one of them knew there would be not one of them left on the rows in a month.** **Not resolved.**
- **The man of forty-nine with the rag and a stick of chalk, who has been writing a levy for nineteen years four times a month and does not know he is writing one, and said *I have four columns to write and the light is going*, and would not say what he heard at a frame on the first of a month.**
- **A woman of sixty-three behind a counter who squares every sheet she is handed with two fingers and does not look at anybody, and a woman of sixty on a bench who squares Ning Yan up once and then looks at the ground.** Neither is spoken to in this batch and neither owes anybody anything.
- **A boy of sixteen keeping one lamp going with about a day and a half of oil in a stone jar under a bench, and a lamp in a cage at the far end of a long table in a room she cannot get into.**
- **A bar across a low door lying on the floor inside a room with the dust broken at one end and not at the other, and a second stair going up out of the far side with seven steps visible and then not.**
- **The figure she decided not to count**, settling out of the ground under her feet and not out of her hands, arriving on no day she can point to.
- **The six terms, standing, with no seventh.** The fifth is unenforceable and both of them know it.
- **The book of eight leaves with five blank leaves that stayed blank**, and she has never called it anything.
- **The four sheets of rice paper in a locked drawer with the key in her bag**, and the forty-one strokes that have not been looked at since the twenty-third of the month beginning 2,024 and are not looked at in this batch.

### What Volume 13 must not inherit as settled

1. **That a number she can work out is hers.** It is a subtraction, the fraction is one in twenty, and there are forty people in a ground of two hundred who can do it. **She worked this out herself in this batch, on the road, on the eighteenth of the month beginning 2,264, and wrote it down as a plain fact and did not argue with it.**
2. **That the third column on the slate is a discovery.** Every man of forty-nine in the county does that column every month of his working life and does not know he is doing it. **He was four feet off her doing it in the first light of the seventh first and she watched and could not tell him.**
3. **That a lamp is the same as being met.** She said the opposite in her own words at ch 703 and nothing in this batch softens it. **What she said at ch 720 is a different and larger thing: there is nothing in that room she can put a figure to, and it is the first time in nineteen years anything has been in front of her that she could not measure, and she went up the step anyway.**
4. **That the announcement was received.** **It is not in the book and it is not on the page.** Four people heard nine seconds of it and no two of them agree and one of them is going to say it wrong for the rest of their life, and she heard it and has told nobody, and she is not going to tell anybody, and the day she tells it is the day there is one account. **No close may resolve what it said.**
5. **That the bar moved.** It is on the floor inside a room with the dust broken at one end and not at the other, and nothing in Volume 12 explains it. **Do not explain it at the close.**

## VOLUME 12 CLOSED — what it resolved, what it did not, and what Volume 13 inherits

**Resolved and closed:** whether a designation made to be harvested can be carried up instead (it can — on paper, ch 717–718) · whether a person can be announced without being designated (no, and the question was wrong, ch 698) · what the eighth-first figure is (218 days, 10d21h36m, ch 717) · what the third column on the slate is (days, one in twenty, ch 701) · what the 2,920 is (ruled out as fifth, page, payday, shortage; attributed to nothing, ch 683–684) · the bill for Court (settled ch 718 on listed work, out of her foundation) · the six firsts she stood at and the two she let pass · the forty-one strokes carried up unlooked-at · the five blank leaves kept blank · the harvest taking a board and never a person.

**Not resolved and still open:** how far the rows sit past the wall (thirteen / sixteen / seventeen on the page; twenty withdrawn as corroborated nowhere) · **the carried county total *thirteen days*, stale in five chapters of Batches 0001–0002 and never settled — the westward leg is exact and the county total is not, and no later phase may settle it by re-deriving a number** · what the money above the Order's building is for · why the bar moved · what the announcement said (four accounts, no fifth) · the fourth name from the bottom on a bench four hundred yards west · the 301 of 912 and what a third of a name is · *twenty-two days*, unplaced · the window, the lime, the hut light · the boy's oil (a day and a half) · the fourteen who can count · the woman of sixty-three's sheet · being findable and what the Scribe does with it (Volumes 12–14's standing bill; the address is Volume 15's) · the moral answer (Volumes 15–16).

**Standing fact, fixed at the Volume 12 close review: the bar is dated nowhere and it was taken up when she was nine.** ch 670 and ch 720 are its two statements and both are ages. **A later phase may not give it a day, a month-begin or a year-count, and may not make her younger or older than nine when it came into her hands.**

**Volume 13, *The Immortal Court*, ch 721–780, inherits exactly this:** she is one step up a second stair with three fingers and a thumb, an open arm, and no instrument for what is above her; a true figure of her is in a book that does not need her alive; whatever is up there knows what she is and what it cost, and does not know her. **Goal fixed by `outline/series.md` §9: *Ning Yan must survive being small in a world of enormous people, or the Court decides what she is for her.* The one and only next phase is `workspace/volume-13-planning/PROMPT.md`.** **Volume 12's live figures after the close review repair: 116,766 words of body prose on method B over sixty chapters at average 1,946 — 29,996 · 28,850 · 29,240 · 28,680 by batch.**

---

## VOLUME 13 BATCH 0001 — ch 721–735, *the bundles* — what the batch added, what is still open, and what Batch 0002 inherits

### What the batch added

- **There is a person in the room and it is the whole of the instrument of the volume.** No name. Both hands on the near edge of the long table. They do not move once in twenty-two days, they do not look up, and **they do not speak in this batch at all.** They are correct and uninformed and neither of those things is a decision.
- **She has counted that room nine times through a grille without the person in it**, because the lamp is on a shelf at the far end and the light goes away from the door. Her instrument works and it was pointed at the wrong end of the room.
- **The second stair goes up and comes back down to the floor of the same room** out of a place in the wall behind the lamp's shelf. That is why seven steps are visible from the door. She counts it to **nine hundred** and loses the count there and **puts the number nowhere.** The whole is nine hundred and four and she does not have it until ch 754. The two stairs are not added and no distance between above the line and below it is stated anywhere in the batch.
- **She can reach the table.** Four days and four tries, and the fourth time she got a hand on the edge and held it for a count of nine, and she gets up on it and goes twenty-two paces along the top of it on her hands and knees with no rail. **She chose not to put a hand on it once while forty boards were on it in front of the person who reads them.**
- **The lamp is not an oil lamp.** No dish, no bowl, no reservoir, no wick, no key, nothing on the shelf, wall behind the light, and **the flame does not lean when her hand passes it sixty times with a door standing open.**
- **The mark is a line cut into the stone of the wall** behind and a hand's breadth to the left of the lamp, the same cut as the table and the shelf. The light stops on it and does not go under it.
- **A bundle comes up the stair on the first.** A man of about forty, on the road a year, about forty boards and a bundle of papers in a cloth, put down in one movement and gone before the table has finished. **The hand goes along them at four to a minute and does not stop: forty boards is ten minutes, the bundle of papers is the other thirty, the whole of a month is forty minutes.**
- **The room does not read boards.** One stroke in the same corner and one figure of the same shape on all forty, read with her fingers in the dark under the table over four days. A figure that comes out the same on forty different boards has not come out of what is on the boards.
- **The deciding takes one look and there is no second one and there is nothing to appeal with and nowhere to stand and wait for one, and the look did not include her.** Nobody refused her. Nobody spoke to her. **And the not writing was not a decision anybody made**, and she said that out loud alone at the near end of the table at the seventh hour of the ninth of the month beginning 2,324, and it is the worst sentence of her life.
- **A thing that arrives with no row is not a thing that has arrived, and there is no procedure for a thing that has not arrived, and there is not one because it has never happened in four hundred years.**
- **The row.** Under the table, flat on stone, face up, a foot long and two inches across. A head, a column, one figure. **Ten days, twenty-one hours and thirty-six minutes, in her own hand**, and the reason is that a figure copied any other way than it was written is not that figure and this room has been putting figures in a column for four hundred years. **So the only thing this building has ever copied perfectly is the price of her.** No name, no place, no date, nothing about what she is.
- **The figure is going.** True as of 2,294 at one hour and twelve minutes a day. **Two days and one hour and twelve minutes out of date at 2,335, three days, three hours and thirty-six minutes at 2,357**, and widening, and **there is not one person in that building who could ever know, because there is not one person above this line who loses a day.** That is the volume's thesis and it is on the page in her mouth.
- **The rate.** Two of her own hand a day against a cut in the wall fifty-eight hands from the top of the cage. **Fifty-eight divided by two is twenty-nine.** Twenty-nine from the first of a month is the thirtieth, and the next turning is on the first of the next, **so there is one day in every month in which that lamp stands on the line with nothing above it and nothing below it, and there has not been one of those days used for four hundred years.** She does not correct the room's arithmetic and cannot add two counts in different kinds of thing.
- **The turnings are not on a schedule.** One came **two days before her own sum said the line would be reached.** The room counts in hands and she counts in days and the two have never agreed.
- **A turning takes eleven seconds and the arm is smaller afterwards by the width of the little finger on her left hand, and the distance the light comes up that wall is the same distance the arm goes down.** The same substance. She does not learn what it is and she does not learn what the lamp is paying for, and **no mouth in Volume 13 may tell her.**
- **The second place at the table**, found at ch 724: a stretch of the far side the length of her forearm, paler and smoother, a shape worn into the wood that is not a mark anybody made with a tool, and nothing in front of it and nothing on it. **She did not touch it, did not name it, and said not one word about it out loud.** It is the first thing in this volume she has left alone on purpose.
- **The bar is left flat against the wall on the left of the room**, by her own hand, on purpose, broken end towards the door, on day 2,344. **The first object of her life she has deliberately left in a room**, said out loud to the bar and not to a person. **And she can go down those eight hundred and eighty steps with nothing in her hands for the first time since she was nine.**
- **SHE HAS SPENT A DAY.** At the fourth hour of 2,357, standing, in daylight, in a room with a person in it, with nobody asked for it, she put her hand up between four bars of iron and closed it on the base of that light and the light went up past the top of the cage and stayed. **The figure she is lighter by steps from seven hundred and ninety-seven to seven hundred and ninety-eight inside one day, and from today it is `D − 1,559` and there is no instrument in this building or in this county or in the world that takes it back.** **Day 2,540 is nine hundred and eighty-one, not nine hundred and eighty.** The mark moved from 2,383 to 2,384 and **that is the first day of slack in a month that has never once been spent in four hundred years, and she spent it, and there is no row in that building with her name at the head of it.**
- **The five blank leaves stayed blank.** She said so out loud at ch 721, standing, in daylight, and did not improve on it, and said it again at ch 735.

### What is still open and was still open before this batch

- **What the lamp is.** No oil, no wick, nothing behind it, it does not lean, a hand turns it in eleven seconds and is smaller afterwards. **Open, and Volume 13 may not close it.**
- **What the lamp is paying for.** Open, and `outline/ending.md` owns it.
- **Why the bar's dust is broken at one end and not at the other.** Named once at ch 721 and **not explained and not resolved.**
- **Why the bar moved at all, and who put it through a barred door.** Open.
- **The second place at the table.** Found, untouched, unnamed, unspoken. **Batch 0003's ninth frame scene at ch 758 is about it and he does not get into it.**
- **Whether the room will ever look at her.** It has not, once, in sixty days.
- **The bundle of papers** under the far end of the table, tied with a cord round twice and not knotted, which she has had her arm into and has not pulled. **She put her fingers under the second turn of the cord and did not pull it.**
- **What is over the second stair.** Nine hundred of it, and then she could not hold the number. The other four steps are hers until ch 754.
- **The boy of sixteen** four miles down this mountain, whose day and a half of oil went out on 2,296, who said he would keep it going after she stopped asking and was right to say it, and who is **not on the page and is not on a stair.** She has not said one word about him out loud.
- **The three figures about her that disagree**: what she has paid, what her own column has taken, and what she has lost. None of them can be checked against another and only one of them is in a hand anyone can read.
- **The woman of sixty on the bench, the man of forty-nine with the rag, the fourth name from the bottom, the fourteen who can count to eighty, the 2,920, the 301 of 912 and what a third of a name is, how far the rows sit past the wall, *twenty-two days* unplaced, the four sheets of rice paper in a locked drawer, the forty-one strokes unlooked at, the six terms with no seventh, the stale county total *thirteen days*, the nine seconds and the four accounts that do not agree.** None of these is on the page in this batch and none is moved by it.
- **Being findable and what the Scribe does with it** — Volumes 12–14's standing bill, the address is Volume 15's. Untouched here and raised by nothing.

### What Batch 0002 must not inherit as settled

1. **That the lamp is fed by oil or by a wick or by anything behind it.** It is not, and no card may say what it is.
2. **That the room reads boards.** It does not, and **the figure at the head of her row therefore did not come off her board.** Whatever the bundle of papers was, that is what the row is made out of, and Batch 0002 has to be able to say it.
3. **That twenty-nine days is a schedule.** It is a descent rate. A hand came two days early and there is no month in it.
4. **That she is being ignored.** **She is not being ignored. She is not being thought about, which is a different thing and much worse, and the difference has to survive into Batch 0002.**
5. **That the day she spent was a payment that was owed to somebody.** It was not. It was the first thing she has ever *spent*, and it bought one day of light in a room where the deciding takes one look, and it cannot be got back, and **it must not be softened, repaid, described as a loan, or treated as recoverable anywhere after ch 735.**
6. **That the bar is behind her.** It is against the left-hand wall of the room she is standing in, where she left it on purpose at ch 731, and it is the only object of her life that is in a room she chose to put it in.
7. **That she can go down the stair.** She has a hand on the table now, the lamp is reachable, and the bag is still at the bottom of the first four flights with the nib and the ink in it, and **she decided at ch 734 that she is not going down.** Batch 0002 may revisit that decision. It must not pretend it was never made.
8. **That anything in this volume has been refused, read, offered or asked.** Nothing has. **A room cannot be asked. It can be paid.**

## VOLUME 13 BATCH 0002 — ch 736–750, *the row* — what the batch added, what is still open, and what Batch 0003 inherits

### What the batch added

- **The boy of sixteen is on the page above the line, and he is the batch's pressure made visible.** He came up eight hundred and eighty steps in the dark at the sixth hour of 2,361 because the lamp on the fourth landing went out on the third of the month beginning two thousand two hundred and ninety-four and there has been no oil in the building for nine years, **and nobody above the line sent him.** He asked one question — why is the lamp at the top of this tower still lit — **and she could not answer it, and said so out loud in that room, and it is the first time in nineteen years a person has come to her with a question and she has had nothing for it.** He went down on 2,387 with her counting all eight hundred and eighty out loud and a number instead of an answer. **He came back up a third time on 2,413** because he has been asked twice, has said he does not know twice, and the man who counts the boards at the far end of that floor has started asking instead. **He is not clever and he is not a voice for anything.** What he can do: get up on a four-foot table in one movement, which she cannot, and count forty boards coming off a table in ten minutes without being told twice. What he cannot do: get under that table, read a strip of wood by touch, or be met where he is standing. **He is below the line for ch 746 and ch 747 and the batch card requires every chapter to show him or show what he cannot do, so both of those chapters now spend one line on it: at ch 746, in her last section, he can get up on that table in one movement and cannot get under it and there is not one thing in that building that would have put a hand under it for him; at ch 747, on the twenty-fifth, he is the only person on either side of the line who could say out loud that he heard her say the two words, and a hand that copies figures exactly is not going to take his word for one word of it.** **Nothing was offered to him in fifteen chapters and nobody above the line was kind to him.**
- **Being small is on the page, shown three ways and never explained.** She can reach the table and has to get up on it on her hands and knees; he gets up on it in one movement. She can get under it; he cannot. She can read a strip by touch; he cannot. **And nothing in that room sees anybody standing on it** — she has been on it twice, he stood on the middle of it for a count of nine, and the person at the table did not look up.
- **There are four rows under that table.** A foot long and two inches across, face up on the stone, square-edged, every one with a head at one end that is a figure and every one with a column. **The column is a column of figures and not a list of things.** Three of them have gone soft at every edge and one of them is hers and is two months old, and she can say they are older by a great deal and cannot say how much and is not going to guess. **Her figure is in her own hand because a figure copied any other way than it was written is not that figure.**
- **THE ROW RULE, found from the furniture at ch 743.** **A hand at a table cannot enter anybody into anything.** There is nothing on that table to write with. A hand that decides what a thing is has to have something to write it down with, and this hand copies exactly and is not allowed to decide anything. **The head of a row is chosen by the person it is about, in another room, standing up, out loud, in daylight, before it is a line anywhere else, and the hand writes what the person says and nothing else.** And **who keeps it: the room stands on it.** The table is for things that arrive and the row is for things that are about people.
- **The rule is four hundred years old and nobody above the line remembers making it and nobody ever will.** There is not one mark in that building that says why a row is entered that way. A rule kept exactly for that long has nobody left in the room who was in it on the day it started. **The reason it exists is the reason she needs it: a hand cannot put a person into a row, so nobody in that building can decide what a person is. And she is never going to be able to ask, because the only way to ask a question in that building is to put it in a row, and she had not got one.**
- **She said a row out loud at 2,393** — one figure, eight hundred and thirty-four, standing, in daylight, in the ordinary voice — **and a hand picked up a board and wrote on it and the hand did not know it was hers.** It is not taken and it is not refused. The board went up that stair on the first of the month beginning two thousand four hundred and fourteen and came back down empty like every other board. **She is not going to find out what is in it for a very long time and she said it anyway.**
- **What a row costs.** **The saying is free and the writing is not, and the cost comes off the hand that writes and not off the person it is about.** Forty boards in ten minutes and a bundle in thirty cost that arm nothing; one figure copied exactly cost the same as one lamp — the width of the little finger on her left hand. She did not take a sixth reading on the day because she was watching the writing, and that is the only thing in ninety-nine days she has got wrong that she could have got right. **And there is no limit on it: she can say a row as many times as she has days for and nobody will stop her and nobody will be angry, and that is the worst thing anybody has ever handed her and it was worked out by her, in four days, off a forearm.**
- **IMMORTAL VESSEL 4 at ch 746, day 2,403, the twentieth of the month beginning 2,384, at one hundred and thirteen days, and its cost named once: she cannot be given a thing.** She is not entered, so there is nothing to hand a stage to, so she got a board out of the stack under the table herself, went up on the table, went twenty-two paces along it, knelt on the shelf and wrote a figure in her own hand on an open arm. **Seven of eleven holds where it was eight.** The interval is said out loud: 78, then 84, then **113, because a stage past the third working point is taken against a row and there was no row to take it against.** No announcement, no sound, no tribulation, and nobody looked up.
- **The one at the table goes out of that room four times in four days** and she is under the table every one of them. **She does not follow and does not stand in the doorway.** She names the carried cost she has not said out loud in nineteen years: a room that is expecting somebody cannot be met by her.
- **`Yuan Sheng` said out loud at the tenth hour of 2,411**, standing, in daylight, in the ordinary voice, on the floor at the near end of the long table. **In the ten seconds before she said it he asked her not to, in the ordinary voice, standing, in daylight, one question — *Is there a way to do this that does not have me in it* — for the first time in nineteen years. She did not answer it and she said it anyway.** The hands came off the near edge for the first time in a hundred and twenty days with nothing on the table. **He did not ask a second time and he did not hide it.** He said what it is and what it costs out loud in the ordinary voice. **She then said the six terms out loud in that room, one at a time, standing, in daylight, as she has said them in every room she has ever said them in, and there was not a seventh one and she did not ask for one.**
- **He is found at ch 749 by the thing that reads records, and nothing speaks to him.** The board went up on the first of the month beginning two thousand four hundred and fourteen. She waited two full days for something and nothing came: no mark on the table, no stroke, no figure anywhere, no hand off the near edge except for the boards. **She is the only person in the world who knows what is in that row and she cannot see it, and she does not tell the boy.**
- **The second place at the table has been noticed by the boy of sixteen** in the last two chapters, standing in a doorway, looking at a room the way a person looks at a room. **She has still not said one word about it out loud and has not touched it and has not named it.** Batch 0003's ninth frame scene at ch 758 is about it and he does not get into it.
- **She has worked out what she would have to do about the light and has not done it.** One day into the cage buys one day; she could hold that lamp off the line for a year out of eight hundred and sixty-one days of her own. **She is not going to, because she has no instrument for what came out and because she has a row in that room and does not know what it is for.** At day 2,420 the lamp is **ten of her own hand below the top of that cage and forty-eight of her own hand off that line**, and it stood on the line at the fourth hour of 2,414, so the twenty-fourth day from day 2,420 is the first of the month beginning 2,444. **CORRECTED AT THE BATCH 0002 REVIEW REPAIR: this line previously said the lamp was ten of her own hand *further down that wall than it was on the first of a month*, which is false and which contradicted ch 750's own sum two sections earlier. A hand went into the cage on 2,414 and it came up past the top, so it has come *down* ten hands since the second and it is forty-eight off the line, not ten below where it was.**
- **The three moving series were stated on the page in words and every one is checkable**: the figure she is lighter by is `D − 1,559`, from 802 at 2,361 to **861 at 2,420**; the staleness of the row is `(D − 2,294) × 72 minutes`, from three days, twenty-one hours, thirty-six minutes at 2,372 to **six days, three hours and thirty-six minutes at 2,417**, which is the day ch 750's closing speech is dated to, and six days, seven hours, twelve minutes at 2,420 is the figure the batch hands to Batch 0003; and her time in that room is `D − 2,301`, from sixty days at 2,361 to **one hundred and nineteen days at 2,420**.

### What is still open and was still open before this batch

- **What the lamp is.** No oil, no wick, nothing behind it, it does not lean, a hand turns it in eleven seconds and is smaller afterwards, and the light falls two of her own hand a day against a line cut in a wall. **Open, and Volume 13 may not close it.**
- **What the lamp is paying for.** Open, and `outline/ending.md` owns it. **No mouth in Volume 13 may tell her.**
- **Why the bar's dust is broken at one end and not at the other.** Named once at ch 721 and **not explained and not resolved in this batch.** The bar is against the left-hand wall of the room she is standing in, where she left it on purpose, and it is the only object of her life that is in a room she chose to put it in.
- **Why the bar moved at all, and who put it through a barred door.** Open.
- **The second place at the table.** Found, untouched, unnamed, unspoken — and now **noticed by a boy of sixteen**, which is not the same thing and does not settle it.
- **What is over the second stair.** Nine hundred of it, and then she could not hold the number. The other four steps are hers until ch 754 and **neither number appears in ch 736–750.** The two stairs are not added anywhere.
- **What was written on the board she got written on at ch 744 and on the board with his name in it.** Both have gone up the stair and both came back down empty. **She is not going to find out for a long time.**
- **What a row that has her in it is for.** **This is the batch's closing question and it is unanswered.** She can enter things and she cannot find out what it does to them, she cannot see either board, she cannot take a line out of a record, and there is not one instrument in that building that can do one single thing about any part of it.
- **The bundle of papers under the far end of the table**, corded round twice and not knotted, which she has had her arm into three times in about a hundred and fifteen days and has not pulled. **She says why out loud: it is the only thing in that room that has not been looked at, and a cord that is not knotted is a thing a hand goes straight into, and a room that has had four hundred years to shut a thing is not a room that shuts things.**
- **What the one at the table goes out of that room to do, four times in four days.** Open, and `outline/volume-13.md` §D6 pins it to Batch 0002 and does not explain it.
- **Why the lamp comes down onto that cut at all.** **CORRECTED AT THE BATCH 0002 REVIEW REPAIR: this bullet previously read *the second time is one day later than the room's own arithmetic and she can see it and is not going to say it to a room of correct people*, and that clause is not supported by any page and never was.** The page reproduces 2,414 exactly and independently three times — **ch 746**, thirty-eight below the top of that cage on 2,404, fifty-eight less thirty-eight is twenty, twenty divided by two is ten days, and the tenth day from that day is the first of the month beginning 2,414 · **ch 747**, forty-six below on 2,408, fifty-eight less forty-six is twelve, twelve divided by two is six days, and the sixth day from that day is the same day · **ch 749**, where it comes down onto the cut and stops on it at the fourth hour of that day and the boy of sixteen is shown standing in front of it. **There is no one-day slip on the page. What is actually open is larger and is what this bullet now carries**: why the light comes down onto that cut at all, **why it does so on the first of a month every thirty days when fifty-eight divided by two is twenty-nine**, why a hand goes into that cage every time it reaches it, and **the fact that no row in that building has a word about any of it.** The one day of slack in every month that has never once been used is on the page at ch 732, she spent it in the month beginning 2,354, and it is not disturbed by any of this.
- **The three figures about her that disagree**: what she has paid, what her own column has taken, and what she has lost. None of them can be checked against another and only one of them is in a hand anybody can read.
- **The woman of sixty on the bench, the man of forty-nine with the rag, the fourth name from the bottom, the fourteen who can count to eighty, the 2,920, the 301 of 912 and what a third of a name is, how far the rows sit past the wall, *twenty-two days* unplaced, the four sheets of rice paper in a locked drawer, the forty-one strokes unlooked at, the six terms with no seventh, the stale county total *thirteen days*, the nine seconds and the four accounts that do not agree.** **None of these is on the page in this batch and none is moved by it.**
- **Being findable and what the thing that reads records does with it** — Volumes 12–14's standing bill, the address is Volume 15's. **Raised at ch 749 and not paid, and the thing does not speak.**

### What Batch 0003 must not inherit as settled

1. **That she is entered and that being entered is a rescue.** She has said a figure out loud in that room and got a figure written down, and **a figure is what a thing costs and not what a thing is.** She has said two words out loud in that room and got them onto a board, and **she cannot see either board and cannot take either one back.** Being entered is not the same as being rescued and this batch says so twice.
2. **That a row costs her.** **It does not. The cost comes off the hand that writes.** Every row in that building for four hundred years has been paid for out of the forearm of the person at that table. She said so out loud at ch 745 and worked out on the same day that **there is no limit on it.**
3. **That there are chairs or that the room is looking for anybody.** There are no chairs and there never have been. The deciding takes one look, there is no second and no appeal, **and nothing in that room sees anybody standing on it.** The person at the table does not turn round, does not look up, did not stop for one figure or forty boards or the fifth hand in four days, **and is not cruel, interested, hesitant or kind.**
4. **That the room reads boards.** It does not. **The figure at the head of her row did not come off her board** — it came off a figure she wrote at two posts before it was ever on wood — and whatever the bundle of papers is, that is what the row is made out of.
5. **That twenty-nine days is a schedule.** It is a descent rate against a cut line, and she spent a day of her own on 2,357 that moved the mark from 2,383 to 2,384, and the light then came down onto the line on 2,384 and again on **2,414**, and it will come down onto it again around **2,444**. The room counts in hands and she counts in days and the two have never agreed.
6. **That the bar is behind her.** It is against the left-hand wall of the room she is standing in, and **Batch 0003 goes down without it** and it is there at the last page of the volume.
7. **That she cannot go down.** **She went down 880 steps on 2,387 and 880 again on 2,417**, and on the first of those she walked past the bag at the bottom of the first four flights, named every object in it out loud, **and said out loud that she decided on 2,356 not to go down for any of them, has not in nine days, is not going to on that day, and has not changed her mind and is not going to pretend she has.** Batch 0003 goes down for it. That decision is revisited and not reversed on the page.
8. **That the lever is available.** It is spent. **The six terms stand and there is no seventh and none may be asked for**, and the sixth was said out loud in that room at ch 748 and the fact that it did not survive being read by a fourth person is left where it landed.
9. **That his being found has been paid.** It has been raised and nothing has spoken to him. **Volume 14 or later, and Volume 13 may not explain it.**

## VOLUME 13 BATCH 0003 — ch 751–765, *the measure* — what the batch added, what is still open, and what Batch 0004 inherits

**Day 2,421 to day 2,480. Fifteen chapters, 31,000 words of body prose on method B, average 2,067.** A stale work-in-progress of ch 751–757 was on the page when the batch opened and was drafted over afresh; **the reasons are in `state/continuity.md` under *VOLUME 13 BATCH 0003*, and three paragraphs of ch 751 were carried across from it.** **The batch has been through its review repair; the record is in `state/continuity.md` under *VOLUME 13 BATCH 0003 — REVIEW REPAIR*, and the Volume 13 close then moved two of its figures again — 30,988 → 31,000 and ch 762 2,076 → 2,088 — recorded under *VOLUME 13 CLOSE — REVIEW REPAIR*.** Batch 0001 is 29,923 and Batch 0002 is 30,152, so the volume is **91,075 over forty-five chapters at average 2,024.** The drafting figures of 30,920 and 91,080 were wrong by arithmetic and both are struck.

### What the batch added

- **THE INSTRUMENT IN THAT ROOM IS A PERSON, and every figure in that room is a comparison and not a measurement.** A row has two ends; the head is a figure; the column is figures; every head-figure was said out loud by a person in some other room and came up a stair in an armful, so it has been moved and not weighed. There is no number in that building for a figure to be more than or less than, **so the only thing a figure in that room can be measured against is the one body at the table, and that body has been the same one for four hundred years.** Every figure that has ever gone into a column there is a statement about that arm. **Nobody in that building has ever been told, and the person at the table has not been told once in four hundred years that every number they wrote down is a number about them.** This is now the volume's second structural fact and it is stated in her mouth at ch 759.
- **She cannot be measured where she stands, and she knows why.** Being looked at is what holds a person still, and there is not one thing in that building looking at her. She manages a count of eleven.
- **The second stair is nine hundred and four, and she has the figure.** Counted down and back up on the twenty-third of the month beginning 2,414 and again going up on the third of the month beginning 2,444. **The four steps she could not hold at ch 723 are the first four out of the floor of the room, not the last four**, and **there is no nine hundred and fifth**, because the stair goes up out of that room and comes back down into the floor of the same room. **She was using the number as a reason for five months and says so out loud.** The two stairs are never added.
- **She has a stub of a nib and a cake of ink**, picked up on the twelfth of the month beginning 2,414 and carried up eight hundred and eighty steps against the fourth rib, and she has written with them twice since: **five on a board at ch 761 and five in her own row at ch 763.** **The ch 734 decision was carried and acted on and not reversed**, and the drawer is a different decision and is still unopened.
- **The mark in that wall falls on a first of a month and not on a thirtieth, for the second time in four hundred years, and it is her own doing.** Twenty-nine days from a turning is a thirtieth when the turning is on a first and a first when the turning is on a second, because of the day she spent into that cage on the fourth of the month beginning 2,354. **What she bought with a day was the loss of the one day of slack that every month in four hundred years has had.** She said it out loud twice and did not tell the room.
- **The room's instrument ran off her arm.** At the ninth hour of the twelfth of the month beginning 2,444 the hands came off the near edge with nothing in front of them, came down the leg of the table, closed on her open right forearm at the wrist and held on the wrist bone for about eleven seconds. **That is the first time in four hundred years the cost of that instrument has come off anything except the person standing at the table**, and it is the width of the little finger on her left hand. **A figure went onto a board and the figure is ten days, twenty-one hours and thirty-six minutes: she said what she was and the room wrote down what she cost.**
- **IMMORTAL VESSEL 5 at ch 761, day 2,461, the eighteenth of the month beginning 2,444, at fifty-eight days, cost named once: she cannot be measured with a person.** Fifty-eight and not a hundred and thirteen, and the reason said out loud: **the first three were taken against a room and a room is not a row.** A comparison takes two things standing still, **and so from today the thing that does the measuring will be her and the thing being measured will be a record, and if there is a person in it, that person is furniture.**
- **THE BATCH'S IRREVERSIBLE STEP, ch 763: two figures in one column.** She took her own strip off the stone under that table for the first time in one hundred and seventy-one days, refused a board out of the stack and said why, **and wrote five under the price, in her own hand, with the nib, standing, in daylight, in the ordinary voice.** Her own forearm went down while the nib was still on the wood, **because the cost of the writing comes off the hand that writes and the hand that wrote it was hers.** She says out loud that a figure that is a price can only be added up and a figure that is a person can only be counted, **that being counted is the thing she has been escaping since she was nine years old, that being entered is not the same as being rescued, and that she did not put it there so that something would come and take it out.**
- **The column cannot be used and cannot be undone.** Ten days, twenty-one hours and thirty-six minutes and five is not a figure. **And the two figures go out of date in different currencies: the price at one hour and twelve minutes a day, and five from the day after the sixth stage, which is a day a hand in the light of that lamp chooses.** Not one instrument in the world puts either of them right.
- **The ninth frame scene is on the page at ch 758 and it is the first one about a place he could get into.** There are two places at that table, one occupied and one **that is the right size for him, that is addressed, and that nobody put him in.** **He does not get into it and he does not say no to it.** Every sentence he speaks in it is in the present tense, no other character is in a marked present frame, and there is no tenth anywhere in ch 721–780. **No figure of what is left of him is stated anywhere.**
- **Ning Wu, once, at ch 765, and it is not a visit.** His name said out loud for the first time in nine years above the line; two days of road and six hundred feet below her; **she says she is not going to, and that it is the first thing she has decided in nineteen years that no mouth in this building asked her about and nothing was going to undo for her.** He settles nothing, he is not on a page of any book, and the year is not named once.

### What is still open and is Batch 0004's

- **What a room does with a column it cannot add up.** This is the batch's closing question and it is unanswered. It is the hinge Batch 0004 turns on: **the room offers to amend the row once at ch 772 and the amendment costs a feed and nobody is lying about any part of it.**
- **What the lamp is, and what the lamp is paying for.** Untouched in this batch. `outline/ending.md` owns the second one and no mouth in Volume 13 may tell her.
- **Why the bar's dust is broken at one end and not at the other.** Named once at ch 721, not explained, not resolved, and **the bar is still against the left-hand wall of that room and was not picked up once in fifteen chapters.**
- **The second place at the table.** She said one word about it out loud for the first time at ch 759 and did not touch it and did not name it. He has now seen it from the inside and has not got into it and has not said no to it. **Volume 14 opens it and Volume 13 does not.**
- **The three boards that have gone up that stair and come back empty**: the stage figure of the fourth of that month in the month beginning 2,384 · the name said on the twenty-eighth of that month · and now, going up on the first of the month beginning 2,504, **the board with the price on it that the room wrote on the twelfth of the month beginning 2,444.** She cannot see any of them and cannot take any of them back.
- **The bundle of papers under the far end of that table**, corded round twice and not knotted, had a hand into three times and not pulled. Not named again in this batch.
- **The locked drawer thirty feet from a copying floor.** **She said out loud at ch 762 that she is not going to open it, and gave a new reason: if there is a page in there with a true line on it, then the last thing in the world that has never been written on is not blank any more, and she would rather be wrong in a column than right on a piece of paper nobody reads.** The five blank leaves stay blank.
- **The bag at the bottom of the first four flights** with the dish, the clay, the ring, the key and the nine inches of hand-made paper. The nib and the ink are gone out of it and against her ribs.
- **The boy of sixteen.** Four miles down that mountain. He has asked twice why the lamp at the top of this tower is lit, he does not know what a row is, he counted forty boards in ten minutes on his own, **and she said out loud at ch 765 that she is not going down to tell him.** Nothing was offered to him in fifteen chapters and he is still below the line at the last page of the batch.
- **Being findable, and what the thing that reads records does with it.** Raised at ch 749, **not paid, not explained and not mentioned again in this batch.** Volume 14 or after it.
- **What is over the second stair.** Nothing. It goes up out of the room and comes back down into the floor of the room and that is the whole of what is above the line she has found in a hundred and seventy-nine days, and it took her four days and nine hundred and four steps to find out that there was nothing on it.
- **The untouched inheritance, all still standing:** the 2,920 and the 301 of 912 · what a third of a name is · how far the rows sit past the wall · *twenty-two days* unplaced · the four accounts of nine seconds and no fifth · the stale county total *thirteen days* · the four erased months of Chapter 3 · the ch 150 refusal · the register still reading *reconciled* and not read · *kiln-born* in two heads and on no paper · **the eleven never raised** · one Lung and one appointment, Feng's, both spent · the Clay Plate still against her ribs and not described · **no office above the Standing Office of the Second Account.**

### What Batch 0004 must not inherit as settled

1. **That being entered is a rescue.** It is not, and it has now been said three times, and the third time is ch 763 and it is not improved on. **She put her own figure in her own row because a price can only be added up and a person can only be counted, and she did it standing up in daylight in the ordinary voice, and she is not going to be let out of it.**
2. **That a row costs her.** It does not, except when she writes it herself. The cost of a figure comes off the hand that writes, and for four hundred years that hand has been at that table. **At ch 763 it came off her, and her forearm is down by the width of the little finger on her left hand and it has not come back.**
3. **That the room can do anything with her.** It measured her once, wrote down what she cost, and made no sound. **It cannot add up her column and there is no instrument in the world that can take a line out of either figure.**
4. **That there are chairs, or that the room is looking for anybody.** There are no chairs and there never have been, the deciding takes one look, there is no second, and **nothing in that room sees anybody standing on it. The person at the table did not look up once in this batch and did not speak once and is not cruel, interested, hesitant or kind.**
5. **That the room reads boards.** It copies figures exactly and does not know whose words it is writing, and **at ch 756 she could not tell which figure cost that arm the little finger, because forty things go up that stair on the first of a month.**
6. **That twenty-nine days is a schedule.** It is a descent rate against a cut line. **Because of one day she spent, the mark has now fallen on a first of a month twice, and Batch 0004's mark falls on the first of the month beginning 2,504.**
7. **That she cannot go down.** She went down eight hundred and eighty steps in the dark, went past the bag, picked up two things out of it, and came back up with no rail on the lower four hundred and forty. She went down nine hundred and four steps in the dark three times and lost a step at the thirty-second turn on one of them. **She has said out loud that she is not going down for the bag again, not for the drawer, and not for the boy.**
8. **That the second stair has anything on it.** It does not. Nine hundred and four is the whole of it and the two ends of it are in the same room.
9. **That the lever is available.** It is spent. **The six terms stand, there is no seventh, none may be asked for, and the fifth term was not spent.**
10. **That his being found has been paid.** It has been raised and nothing has spoken to him. **Volume 14 or later, and Volume 13 may not explain it. There is no tenth frame scene.**

## VOLUME 13 BATCH 0004 — what the batch added, what it closed, and what Volume 14 inherits

### What Batch 0004 closed

1. **What a room does with a column it cannot add up. CLOSED at ch 770.** A room's only answer is to look at it once and then wait. The look happened at ch 769 over a foot of pale wood on the top of the table, it wrote the head figure again in the column and the head figure, **and not one stroke in the second pair of cuts, and there is not one instrument in the world that reports what a look saw.** The answer to the question she has been carrying since the seventh of the month beginning 2,474 is: *it does the only thing a room does, and then it does nothing, and it is not going to ask her about it again.*
2. **The price board that was going up the stair on 2,504. CLOSED at ch 772.** It went up in an armful of forty with a second board carrying the same figure that the room wrote four days earlier, four minutes apart, and she could not tell which was which and neither could anybody else. **The two-figure problem has not become a problem for the room, because the room cannot hold an error and she cannot get at a record.**
3. **The refusal of the amendment. CLOSED at ch 777, and it is not out of pride and she says so out loud so that it is on the record in her own mouth.** The figure it buys is the room's and is one she has never seen; the figure she wants standing is hers and is wrong the moment after it is written. **And the reason is the fourth of the six terms: there has not been one line in that building for two hundred and twenty-eight days that got into a row that she had not said out loud there first, and the room has never amended a row, and there is no instrument in the world that amends a row twice, so she would never be able to check it once.**
4. **Whether she can stand still. CLOSED at ch 775.** She cannot, and she could not, and the reason is not that she is better at it: **being looked at is what holds a person still, and there is not one thing in that room looking at her, and it turns out that is not the only way.** She gets a count of eleven out of it four times in a row on a stone floor with nothing in the room, and **the only difference is that she has something to be still for.**

### What Batch 0004 added and Volume 14 inherits

1. **The lamp is out and she put it out, and there is nothing in the world that can put it back.** Six days of dark at the last page, a room that has never had to decide anything in the dark, and **a row on a strip of pale wood under that table that says what she is, written by her, true, and no instrument in the world can read one word of it.** Volume 14 opens in that dark unless it opens it out, and the batch does not say who could.
2. **The second place at that table, noticed twice.** He has seen it from inside once and has neither got into it nor said no to it. **The boy of sixteen found it in the doorway at ch 780 and said out loud that it is not a hand, and she told him out loud that it is not a chair and that it is the right size for somebody who is not here.** `outline/volume-13.md` §D8 reserves this for Volume 14 and Volume 13 does not open it. **The boy is the only mortal in the volume and he is the only person who has ever asked a question in that room and got an answer, and the answer was not the one he came for.**
3. **She said his name out loud a second time, in the dark, in the middle of the six terms, on purpose, knowing what a hand does with what it reads.** Nothing in that room made a sound. **There is not one thing in that room she can tell has heard anything, and she is not going down two days of road to find out what was written.** **This is the engine Volume 14 is handed and it is the same engine the ch 748 name was, with the light now out, and it is the question Volume 14 opens onto.**
4. **The second of the six terms cannot be kept in that room any more.** She said it out loud for the last time there at the ninth hour of the twenty-ninth of the month beginning 2,504 and **the record she has kept in the ordinary voice, standing up, in daylight, in nineteen years has lost the room it was kept in.** Volume 14 has to decide what a record is when the light it was made in is gone, and Volume 13 does not decide it.
5. **The six terms stand and there is no seventh and none may be asked for. The lever is spent and was carried zero times in this batch. The fifth term was not spent. The asking at ch 665 is untouched.**
6. **The second of the terms is *in daylight* and the third is *standing up* and the sixth is *I decide everything*, and she has said the sixth out loud in that room three times in this batch and has not had to decide anything in it since, because there is no light in it to decide anything in.**
7. **The immovable object in the batch is that she cannot be looked at again, and a sixteen-year-old boy looked at her six days later and nothing in the room decided anything.** That is the sentence Volume 14 can be built on and it is on the page at ch 780.
8. **She is not going down to tell him what a row is, and she said so out loud on the sixth of the month beginning 2,474 and again on the twenty-ninth of the month beginning 2,504, and she did not take it back, and she told him what a row is on the fifteenth of that month to a landing nobody was on and on the seventh of the month beginning 2,534 to a boy who came up eight hundred and eighty steps for a sentence and did not want a definition.** She has now told him twice and has still not gone down to him, **and that is the state Volume 14 inherits: she tells people at a distance and not at a door.**
9. **What the one at that table looks like from the outside.** It spoke once in sixty chapters, at ch 772, it is correct, and **nobody in the volume has ever been in a position to describe it and it has never been described.** `outline/volume-14.md`'s planner owns that.
10. **Merit settles once in the volume, at ch 779, on the page, off the ground the room stands on, never on a death, with no figure of it stated.** **The bundle of papers under the far end of that table is against the third leg of that table and was against the second, and she says one sentence about it and does not look for an instrument.** Nothing about his bill at ch 749 is paid and nothing about it may be paid by this handoff.

### What the Volume 13 close must audit and must not re-open

- **Every figure in the four series** — `D − 1,559`, `D − 2,301`, `(D − 2,294) × 72 minutes` and the lamp at two of her own hand a day from a feed — **against the day its speech is dated, by script, across all sixty chapters.**
- **`grep -oiw court` over `chapters/volume-13/*.md` must return exactly 1**, and it is at ch 772.
- **`grep -oi forgiv`, `grep -oi "the year"`, `grep -oi thank` over `chapters/volume-13/*.md` must each return 0.**
- **`grep -oi "twenty-two days"` must return 0.** Two hits were written in the Batch 0004 first draft as the substring in *two hundred and twenty-two days* and were cut before the batch closed; **a drafter of Volume 14 must not put it back.**
- **`grep -h '^## ' chapters/volume-13/*.md | sort | uniq -d` must be empty**, and `comm -12` of the sixty chapter titles against volumes 01–12 must be empty.
- **The bar is dated nowhere and must stay that way.** She took it up when she was nine. **It is against the left-hand wall of that room at the last page and Volume 14 inherits it there.**
- **How far the rows sit past the wall is not stated and Volume 14 may not state it.** **No distance between anything above the line and anything below it may be stated in any unit in any direction.**
- **The two stairs stay two stairs.** Eight hundred and eighty in two runs of four hundred and forty, and nine hundred and four with two ends in the same room, and they are never added.
- **The lamp is out and nothing in the volume can relight it. Volume 14 may not relight it in its first chapter without an on-page reason and a cost.**

## VOLUME 13 CLOSE — what Volume 14 inherits

**Volume 13 is closed, measured and audited. `ch 721–780`, sixty chapters, 118,951 words of body prose on method B at an average of 1,983, day 2,301 to day 2,540. The full measurement is `state/continuity.md` under *VOLUME 13 — THE CLOSE* and the cold-reader position is `state/current.md` under *Who is where at the end of Volume 13*. Volume 14 is *The Scribe's Apprentice*, ch 781–840, and `outline/series.md` §9 fixes its goal in one sentence: *In this volume, Ning Yan must refuse the thing Yuan was offered and refused, or the whole bargain survives.* **This block hands over what is on the page and stops. It does not plan Volume 14.**

### The four things Volume 14 inherits as facts, and not as a prompt to a plot

1. **She has bought a row in the dark with a lamp she cannot feed and a day she cannot get back.** The head of the row says what she has paid — ten days, twenty-one hours and thirty-six minutes, put there by her own hand on the first of the month beginning 2,294 — and under it, in the second pair of cuts down, is a five in her own hand, and under that, twelve pairs of cuts that are empty. **There is no light in the room to read either figure and there is not one instrument in the world that could read either of them.** **The price at the top has been getting more wrong every day since a month she can name, and there is nobody above the line who could ever know, because above the line nobody loses a day.** The Court did not decide what she is because she turned the lamp off herself, and that is a thing she did and can do again, **and it is the first thing in thirteen volumes that has been a purchase rather than a loss.**
2. **A thing which reads records has read a name she said out loud, in a room, on purpose, knowing what a hand does with what it reads — and it has not spoken.** She said it once at the tenth hour of the twenty-eighth of the month beginning 2,384 at ch 748, and once again on the fifth of the month beginning 2,534 at ch 780 in the middle of the six terms. **The light in that room went out on the first of that month, and there is not one thing in that room she can tell has heard anything, and she has said out loud that she is not going down two days of road to find out what was written.** **And the second of the six terms is *in daylight*, and she said it out loud in that room for the last time at the ninth hour of the twenty-ninth of the month beginning 2,504. Volume 14 inherits a record whose second term cannot be kept where it was made.**
3. **There are two places at that table and one of them is the right size for him and nobody put him in it.** Seen from inside once, at ch 758, with neither a yes nor a no. **And noticed for the second time in the volume at ch 780, by a boy of sixteen standing in a doorway who looked at a room the way a person looks at a room and who does not know what it is, and who said *that is worn by something being put in the same place a great many times over a very long time and it is not a hand*.** `outline/volume-13.md` §D8 reserves it for Volume 14 and Volume 13 did not open it.
4. **She cannot be looked at again, and a boy looked at her sixteen days after she found that out, and nothing in the room decided anything when he did.** She found out at ch 776 on day 2,524, the twenty-first of the month beginning 2,504. He looked at her at ch 780 on day 2,540, the seventh of the month beginning 2,534, and ch 780 says *the first time since the ninth hour of the twenty-first of the month beginning two thousand five hundred and four* in its own words. **2,540 − 2,524 = sixteen.** **The six is the lamp's outage, 2,534 to 2,540, and it is a different six and must not be used here.** That sentence is on the page and is the hinge Volume 14 can be built on.

### The four things Volume 14 may not inherit as settled

1. **That she is going to be rescued. Nobody in Volume 13 offered it, nobody above the line sent that boy, and she says out loud at ch 762 and ch 763 that she is not going to be rescued out of it and she is not going to be let.**
2. **That the second place is a chair. It is not a chair, it is not a seat, it is addressed, and nobody put him in it.**
3. **That the lever is available. It is spent, the six terms stand and there is no seventh, the fifth term was not spent, and the asking at ch 665 is untouched and is not treated as retractable anywhere.**
4. **That the lamp can be relight. Nothing in Volume 13 can relight it. Any Volume 14 chapter that lights it owes the reader an on-page reason and a cost.**

### Four defects this close found, measured and recorded, that Volume 14's first batch must not inherit silently

**These are on the committed page and are handed over rather than rewritten because a close is not a drafting pass. They are arithmetic and they are checkable, and Volume 14 may quote the corrected figure or the uncorrected one, but it must not quote a fifth.**

1. **ch 764's *the first time in a hundred and seventy-eight days that I have had something in this room that is mine* is unresolvable from the page and was left standing.** The section is dated the twenty-eighth of the month beginning 2,444, which is day 2,471, and the figure matches no anchor in the volume: 2,471 − 2,294 (the day the strip of pale wood came up that stair) = 177 · 2,471 − 2,344 (the day she left the bar against the wall, which is what the two sentences before it are about) = 127 · 2,471 − 2,465 (the day she wrote the five) = 6 · 2,471 − 2,301 = 170. **Volume 14 must not quote 177, 127, 178 or 170 as the length of that interval, and must not build on it.**
2. **ch 766's sixteen days is nineteen.** The chapter opens *She put five into a column on the twenty-second of the month beginning two thousand four hundred and forty-four, and on the eleventh of the month beginning two thousand four hundred and seventy-four she took the strip of pale wood back out … because sixteen days is long enough.* **The twenty-second of the month beginning 2,444 is day 2,465 and the eleventh of the month beginning 2,474 is day 2,484, and the interval is nineteen days, not sixteen.** The number *sixteen* is separately correct elsewhere in the same chapter — at the eighth, day 2,481, she says *the five has been in that column sixteen days*, and 2,481 − 2,465 = 16 ✓ — so the chapter carries two different anchors under one figure. **Three places say sixteen where the span is nineteen: the chapter title *Sixteen Days Under That Table*, the opening sentence, and the section heading *What Sixteen Days Is*.** Volume 14 does not have to touch this chapter, but nothing in Volume 14 may restate the span as sixteen.
3. **ch 734's chapter title is *What A Hand Is Charged* and `grep -oi charged` over the volume returns 1,** against a construction budget written at 0. **The word is the commercial one — the room charges the hand that writes, one figure for the width of the little finger on her left hand, which is the volume's own economics and is on the page at ch 745 — and it is not the Volume 12 surface answer and it does not gesture at Volumes 15–16's moral answer.** Recorded rather than renamed.
4. **`grep -oi week` over the volume returns 1,** at ch 735, in the ordinary duration *that has not changed in four weeks*, against a budget written at 0. **It is not a named week and it is not a dated interval.** Recorded rather than rewritten.

### What the Volume 13 close's review repair moved, so Volume 14 does not inherit a figure twice

**`logs/volume-13-close.review.log` found six figures and six dates on the page that the close had got wrong or left wrong, and all twelve are now correct. Nothing else moved and no plot changed. The full table is in `state/continuity.md` under *VOLUME 13 CLOSE — REVIEW REPAIR*. Four of them are a trap for the next batch and are named here:**

1. **The bar went down on the twenty-first of the month beginning 2,324, which is day 2,344, and not on the twenty-first of the month beginning 2,354.** ch 731 stages it across nine dated beats all on that day and ch 720, the last chapter of Volume 12, already has it against that wall on day 2,300; the six pages that said 2,354 were copies made in Batches 0002–0004 of a figure the staging chapter never wrote, and all six are corrected. **At the last page of Volume 13, day 2,540, the bar has been against that wall for 196 days, and a Volume 14 chapter that quotes the interval must derive it from day 2,344.**
2. **ch 764 line 81 now reads *a hundred and twenty-seven days*,** which is 2,471 − 2,344. It said a hundred and seventy-one, which was the strip-of-pale-wood interval copied out of ch 763. **Volume 14 may not quote 171 for the bar.**
3. **ch 764 line 87 still reads *a hundred and seventy-eight days* and is still unresolvable,** for the reason given above. **Volume 14 must not quote 127 there either**, even though 127 is now the correct bar interval — the figure on that page is 178 and it is not derived.
4. **ch 747 line 47 now reads *eight hundred and forty-seven days*** at day 2,406 by `D − 1,559`, and ch 747 line 19 reads *eight hundred and forty-four days* at day 2,405 by the aperture anchor, which opened on the eighteenth of the month beginning 1,544, day 1,561. **Two different series, two different offsets, two days apart, and both are correct; a reader who assumes they are the same series will find a discrepancy that is not there.**

### What Volume 14 may not do, restated once and not twice

**No bracket** — the layer is not in the room and Volume 14 inherits that argument · **no clearance, seal or countersignature named or implied** · **`Ascendant` 0 and `Choir` 0, and `Court` may be used again on Volume 14's own budget** · **nothing that explains what the lamp above the line is for, because that is the moral answer and `outline/ending.md` owns it** · **no preview of the Anchor, the Sisterhood, the bargain or the Scribe's terms** · **no office above the Standing Office of the Second Account** · **no figure of what is left of him** · **no thanking, and `thank` 0** · **the year of non-forgiveness still running and `forgiv` 0 and `the year` 0** · **no harem and nothing a romance, and she never says her mother's name** · **MEMBER ABSENT not raised** · **the eleven never raised, and the four erased months of Chapter 3 never named, and the ch 150 refusal never softened** · **one Lung and one appointment, Feng's, both spent, Feng dead and on no page** · **the four accounts of nine seconds and no fifth** · **the 2,920 and the 301 of 912 unattributed and unmentioned, and what a third of a name is is open** · **how far the rows sit past the wall is open and Volume 13 does not settle it** · **the Order is not a conspiracy and not a chamber and is not on the page** · **the window a hundred yards off the east side, the lime off the bar at ch 604 and the hut light stay unspent** · **the bar is dated nowhere and its dust is broken at one end and is not resolved** · **and the two stairs stay two stairs: eight hundred and eighty in two runs of four hundred and forty, and nine hundred and four, never added.**

## VOLUME 14 PLANNING REVIEW REPAIR — two threads corrected and one added (15 Oct 2026)

**The plan asserted two things about the second place at that table that the page contradicts. Both are
corrected here, and a third thread the plan had missed is opened. No chapter prose exists.**

- **CORRECTED — "the boy does not know about the second place" was false, and the thread's shape changes
  with it.** At ch 780:93–107 he said *there is a place worn in it*, and she told him out loud, standing,
  in the dark, in the ordinary voice, that **it is not a chair, it is not a seat, it is addressed, it is
  the right size for somebody who is not here, not one instruction in this room put anybody in it, and she
  found it on the ninth of the month beginning 2,384 and has not touched it** — and that she **cannot tell
  him what it is for.** He has it. He has had it in her own words since the seventh of the month beginning
  2,534. **So the thread is no longer *whether he finds out there is a second place*. It is *who it is the
  right size for*, and he has not asked, and she has said she cannot tell him.** Batch 0002's *the seat*
  and Batch 0003's *the keeping* both run on that, and neither was re-planned. **The thread as it stood
  before this repair is struck and is not to be answered by any card in Batch 0001.**
- **ADDED, AND CORRECTED ONCE — the frame scene at ch 758 is Yuan's, not the boy's.** Ch 758 is dated the
  fifth of the month beginning 2,444, it is a frame scene away from her hearing, and the shape in that wood
  is **his** — Yuan's — and he says on the page: *That is mine. I know it is mine the way I know my own
  count is mine.* **The boy of sixteen was not in that scene and does not know it happened; his own two
  notices of the second place are ch 749 and ch 780.** A first draft of this repair wrongly handed the boy
  that scene. It is struck, and no card may give him Yuan's knowledge, Yuan's shape or Yuan's line.
- **ADDED — WHEN SHE FOUND THE PLACE IS OPEN AND THREE ANCHORS DISAGREE.** She says at **ch 780** that she
  found it on the ninth of the month beginning 2,384, which is **day 2,392**. The page stages the discovery
  itself at **ch 724, at the sixth hour of the twenty-third of the month beginning 2,294 — day 2,316,
  seventy-six days earlier**, where she looks along the far side, stops counting, and says *it is not a
  chair*. **Ch 758's own figure of a hundred and forty-eight days since she said one word about it out loud
  resolves from a third anchor again.** **No card in any batch may state a day for the finding, may do the
  subtraction, or may treat any one of the three as settled** — this is the failure the Volume 13 close
  recorded against the bar's put-down day, where a close canonised one chapter's figure without noticing
  that another chapter disagreed.
- **UNCHANGED — what the thread still is.** The second place at that table has been **seen from inside
  once, by Yuan at ch 758, and noticed twice by the boy, at ch 749 and ch 780; the boy has still neither got
  into it nor said no to it, and she has still not refused it and has still said out loud only that she
  cannot say what it is for.** Not addressed in Batch 0001. Addressed and refused at ch 822, said, and at ch 838, sent. The
  place stays empty and addressed at the last page of the volume.

## VOLUME 14 BATCH 0001 CLOSED at ch 795 — ten open threads carried into Batch 0002

1. **THE OIL, AND WHO OWNS THE LIGHT.** Three lamps in rotation; he is on three nights out of six and two
   hours on each. **From ch 795 the lamp is standing on the top of that table and the dark in that room is
   owed rather than hers, and she cannot hold it, and there is no oil in this building and none on this
   mountain.** Open: what happens when his store runs to the place where the next first of a month is
   unreachable. **No burn rate, no exhaustion date and no cups figure exists on the page and none may be
   invented in Batch 0002.**
2. **THE SECOND PLACE.** **Unchanged by this batch.** She has told him what she can (not a chair, addressed,
   the right size for somebody who is not here, put there by no instruction, she cannot say what it is for)
   and at ch 795 she adds only that a lamp at the near end does not change one thing about where it is.
   **Not addressed, not refused, not occupied. Addressed and refused at ch 822, said, and at ch 838, sent.**
3. **WHO WROTE HER RECORD.** Two hands: hers and one at the far end of that table. **New at ch 789** — she has
   been saying the contents of that wood out loud for thirty-one days in sentences and **has never seen a
   single cut on it**, and it is not letters, it is cuts, and **a thing made of cuts can be read by somebody
