   who cannot read.** Open: what the head figure is written *as*, and whether the twelve empty pairs were cut
   in advance or cut for something that did not happen. **She gave two answers at ch 789 and took the
   question away with her.**
4. **A FIGURE IN SOMEONE ELSE'S HEAD.** **New and irreversible at ch 793.** Seven is in a boy of sixteen's
   head. **He is not bound, not entered, not sent**, he learned the word *column* off the wood on the eighth
   and used it correctly on the fifth, and **there is not one instrument above this line that will ever find
   out what he does with it.** She has said out loud that she will not ask.
5. **THE VOICE, HEARD BY A PERSON.** **New at ch 793.** She said Yuan's objection out loud, in the room, in
   front of him, and gave his name, and told him the voice can hear him. **He has not yet asked it a
   question and she has not yet answered one in front of the boy.** Open for Batch 0002: whether that
   continues. **The tenth frame scene is ch 812 and he does not get into it.**
6. **THE STAGE, AND WHY IT WENT IN WHERE IT DID.** Seven went into the third pair of cuts, the first of the
   twelve empties. **She answered her own ch 789 question with it, and the answer is hers and not a rule.**
   IV8 is at ch 809 on day 2,666 at sixty-eight days, taken against a row with two figures in it, and its cost
   is **she cannot be unread where she stands.**
7. **THE BUNDLE UNDER THE FAR END.** Corded round twice and not knotted; **dust on the top of it with the
   shape of the cord lying in it, which means it has not been touched in as long as the dust has been there.**
   She has had her arm in it five times and has never pulled. **She decided at ch 790 not to open it
   *tonight* and said the word out loud on purpose. Open.**
8. **THE BEARER.** Nine hundred and four steps once a month with forty boards under one arm, in the dark,
   for four hundred years, **never looking at one of them, and now known by her to be walking a stair in the
   dark with no light on the boards he is carrying.** Open.
9. **THE DAY SHE FOUND THE PLACE.** Still open, three anchors still disagree (her ch 780 figure, the ch 724
   staging, ch 758's own). **No chapter in Batch 0001 stated a day, did the subtraction, or treated any one
   of the three as settled.**
10. **WHAT THE LAMP GOING OUT MEANS.** She put the cage lamp out at ch 778 and has not fed it since, and the
    room's own clock has struck on nothing every month since. **Ch 795 leaves the cage out at sixty-six days.**
    **Nothing in this batch relit it and nothing in Batch 0002 may either.**

**Irreversible, closed, do not re-open:** she emptied his lamp into one that stays and sent the vessel down
the stair empty, and **the dark in that room is now owed and cannot be un-let, and she has said she would
not have it back if it were offered** · her own strip of pale wood is off the floor of that table and has not
gone back · seven is in the third pair of cuts in her own hand · she cannot be hidden by the dark · she has
been to the bottom of her own stair in light and did not count it, and said why.

**Not to be named in Batch 0002:** the moral answer · the Anchor · the tax · what the walls are for ·
the bargain's mercy · anything a thing above the room is doing · the third hand's board before ch 810 ·
the terms before ch 826 · the price before ch 826 · the address before Volume 15.

**Re-measured after the 15 Oct 2026 review, nothing here moved.** The repair closed ten defects and none of
them touched a thread. Two of them bear on what Batch 0002 inherits and are recorded here so the next drafter
has them in the same place.

1. **THE CONDITION SHE SET IS NOW SPENT AS A CONDITION AND NOT AS A RULE.** At ch 791 she said out loud that
   **if there is no lamp in this room on the fifth of the month beginning 2,594 then she does not take the
   seventh stage**, and at ch 793 the condition came within a count of a hundred and forty of being true:
   **two hours of that night she stood four feet off that table in the dark with no oil in the building for
   nine years and none on the mountain and no figure anywhere that said the lamp would come up eight hundred
   and eighty steps.** He arrived about a count of a hundred and forty past the eighth hour. **The condition
   has now been tested once, in front of a person, and held. It is not a rule and it is not a precedent; it
   is one night. Batch 0002 may put the supply in front of her again and it may not restate this one.**
2. **NO BURN RATE, NO EXHAUSTION DATE, NO CUPS FIGURE EXISTS ON THE PAGE, AND NONE MAY BE INVENTED.** The
   review's repair to ch 793 states the supply problem in the only terms available — no oil in the building,
   none on the mountain, a lamp arrives by hand up eight hundred and eighty steps or it does not — **and
   deliberately stops short of a figure.** **That boundary still holds for Batch 0002**, whose firsts of a
   month are 2,624, 2,654 and 2,684. **What is on the page and may be relied on: from ch 795 the light in
   that room is owed and not hers, the vessel went down the stair empty, and a lamp has to be carried up.**

## VOLUME 14 BATCH 0002 CLOSED at ch 810 — the threads this batch moved, opened, or closed

**Ten threads carried in from Batch 0001 at ch 795, and what ch 796–810 did to each. Days 2,601 to 2,684, fifteen
chapters, 28,862 words of body prose on method B, average 1,924, zero bracketed layer lines. Full record in
`state/continuity.md` under ***VOLUME 14 BATCH 0002 — ch 796–810, \*the seat\****.**

1. **THE OIL, AND WHO OWNS THE LIGHT — still open, and the batch did not pretend otherwise.** Three lamps in
   rotation. **No burn rate, no cups figure, no hour the lamp runs out and no exhaustion date exists on the page and
   none was invented; a draft line at ch 809 carrying *about three hours of it was already gone* was cut before the
   chapter closed.** What is on the page: the lamp stands on the top of the table, the light goes a hand's width
   along the near end and stops, **the unit of measurement is the width of her own hand** (ch 796), the vessel went
   down the stair empty at ch 795, and a lamp has to be carried up eight hundred and eighty steps.
   **New at ch 796: she asked him for a night and it took four seconds, and at ch 802 she found out she has been
   agreed to in advance for about three weeks without knowing, which she says is worse than being asked and then
   says she would rather have.** Open for Batch 0003: the same open question — what happens when his store runs to
   the place where the next first of a month is unreachable — and nothing about it may be answered with a figure.
2. **THE SECOND PLACE — advanced, not answered.** **She got light onto it at ch 805, from about four feet off, at
   the ninth hour of the ninth, having carried the lamp twenty-two paces and brought her own record with her
   because she would not leave a figure of hers in the dark. The finding is one sentence: it is not the shape of a
   person sitting in it.** She describes only what she can stand behind — deeper at the front than at the back, not
   deeper in the middle than at the edges — and says out loud that she will not guess. **She also corrects a thing
   she said to him at ch 780: that it had been worn by somebody sitting in it.** **She has told no one the finding.**
   **New at ch 804: she lost the right to say out loud, standing in that room, *I do not know what that is for*,
   because at ch 803 she told the far end of that table what she knows about what it is working to.** Addressed and
   refused at ch 822, said, and ch 838, sent. The place stays empty and addressed at the last page of the volume.
3. **WHO WROTE HER RECORD — advanced twice and still open.** **At ch 801 she worked out what a head figure is for,
   out loud, from the shape of her own wood: it is the figure everything underneath it is read against, which makes
   it a price, and a price does not date and does not add up. So her column is a column of amounts and not a
   record of what happened, the five and the seven are amounts against ten days, twenty-one hours and thirty-six
   minutes, and the empty pairs are entries in a column of amounts rather than nights — nine of them until ch 809,
   which wrote the eighth into the fourth pair and left eight.** **New at ch 798 and ch 800: a paragraph of words in
   her own handwriting is in the stack of forty boards and she cannot tell which of the forty it is and cannot find
   out whether that room reads words or only cuts. At ch 810 the room copied a third hand's board exactly, which
   is the first evidence on the page that it reads what it is not given.** She has said she is not going to write to
   the price. Open.
4. **A FIGURE IN SOMEONE ELSE'S HEAD — grew by one, and the boy is still not bound, not entered, not sent.** **He
   now has eleven, given to him at ch 800 as the only count she had never said out loud.** **And at ch 807 he has
   a line in the record that is not hers — the fact of his own hand going into the bundle under the far end of that
   table on the eleventh of that month and coming out without pulling — and there is no place in that building for
   it and she is not going to make one.** **The finding she cannot put anywhere: he did not ask her permission, and
   she found out he had not asked on purpose.** Open, and Batch 0003 runs the chapter where he says yes and
   everything after it is done inside a record with three hands in it.
5. **THE VOICE, HEARD BY A PERSON — the objection is spent once and it is the boy's, not his.** **At ch 808 the
   boy of sixteen objected, out loud, in the ordinary room and in her own hearing, and his objection was the right
   one: a column with a price at the head is a column of amounts, so the column is reading a count she made against
   a price she cannot read, and the only way of finding out what the column is doing is to use it. She took the
   stage anyway.** **CORRECTED IN THE REVIEW PASS: an earlier record of this thread and of Batch 0002's card
   credited this objection to Yuan and said he spoke in her own mouth. He did not speak in ch 796–810 at all — there
   is no frame scene in that batch — and nothing on the page says he did.** His one speech in Volume 14 is at ch 791,
   in her own hearing and not in a marked frame, and it bought nothing. **The tenth frame scene is ch 812 and he does
   not get into it and nothing in Batch 0003 may have anything speak to him that is not her.**
6. **THE STAGE, AND WHY IT WENT IN WHERE IT DID — the seventh stage's answer is superseded by the eighth's.**
   **Eight went into the fourth pair of cuts at ch 809, at sixty-eight days, on the thirteenth of the month
   beginning 2,654, in oil light at the near end of that table, in her own hand, read out loud first.** The
   interval is fixed by arithmetic and said as arithmetic: **a stage taken against a row with two figures in it has
   to be taken against both of them, and one of them is a count she wrote on the fifth of that month, and a count
   already written never comes right again and there is no day to wait for.** **The cost of the eighth stage, named
   once: she cannot be unread where she stands.** **IV9 is at ch 828, day 2,732, the nineteenth of the month
   beginning 2,714, at sixty-six days, and its cost is that she cannot be alone in the room any more.**
7. **THE BUNDLE UNDER THE FAR END — decided twice and closed for this batch.** **At ch 806 she did not open it and
   said the word *tonight* out loud on purpose, in light, for the second time in her life, and spent a count of a
   hundred and forty doing nothing in front of a person.** **At ch 807 she gave the boy nothing and said why: a
   person who can be given a thing can be given a job, and she has not asked for one and is not going to. He went
   under that table on his own account with nothing in it that she had sent him, found the two turns himself, and
   did not pull.** **The bundle has been touched by a second pair of hands. It has not been opened, the cord is not
   knotted, the dust is undisturbed, and it is not pulled.** Open, and Batch 0003 may not open it before the place
   is refused.
8. **THE BEARER — advanced, and the finding is one sentence.** **At ch 803 he came up nine hundred and four steps
   in the dark and put forty boards on a table with a flame burning a foot in from the near end of it and did not
   look at it** — not at the top of the stair, not in the doorway, not while walking past it with forty boards
   under his arm. **And a hand went along that table afterwards, forty strokes, four to a minute, the same stroke,
   starting in the same place on every board, and none of them stopped.** Open.
9. **THE DAY SHE FOUND THE PLACE — still open, three anchors still disagree.** **No chapter in ch 796–810 stated a
   day for the finding, did the subtraction, or treated any one of the three as settled.**
10. **WHAT THE LAMP GOING OUT MEANS — untouched.** **The cage lamp is out for a hundred and fifty days at ch 810
    and nothing in this batch relit it, fed it, or discussed it as relightable.**

**NEW AND IRREVERSIBLE AT CH 810, CLOSED AND NOT TO BE RE-OPENED.** **A board came up that stair on the first of
the month beginning 2,684 with his name on it in a hand that is not hers and not the room's, and the room copied
it exactly into the column at the far end of that table, stroke for stroke, and did not stop on it. He is readable
by the thing that made him and that cannot be put back.** **The bill raised at ch 749 and unpaid across four
hundred chapters is paid at ch 810 and not explained here; the address is Volume 15's per `outline/series.md` §7
milestone 8.** **She said his name out loud in that room three times — at the tenth hour of the twenty-eighth of the
month beginning 2,384, on the seventh of the month beginning 2,534, and on the fifth of the month beginning 2,594 in
front of the boy — and one of those three went with a boy in the room and one went with a figure of hers and one
went the length of that table.** **No one said the word for the third hand. There is no line in brackets. No face
and no voice. The board did not go back down that stair.**

**Not to be named in Batch 0003:** the moral answer · the Anchor · the tax · what the walls are for · the bargain's
mercy · anything a thing above the room is doing · the place described as kept before ch 818 · the terms or the
price before ch 826 · **anything that speaks to him that is not her** · any second look · any second occasion for
the one look spent at ch 776.

## VOLUME 14 BATCH 0003 CLOSED at ch 825 — the open threads, the irreversible list, and what Batch 0004 may not inherit as clean prose

**Batch 0003 is ch 811–825, day 2,685 to day 2,721, pressure *the keeping*, 30,995 words of body prose on method B
at average 2,065, one midpoint at ch 812, one irreversible step at ch 822, zero bracketed layer lines in fifteen.
Full record in `state/continuity.md` under ***VOLUME 14 BATCH 0003 — ch 811–825, \*the keeping\****.**

**THE TEN OPEN THREADS, as the page carries them at ch 825.**

1. **THE FAIR HAND UNDER HER COLUMN — new in this batch, and the volume's damage.** From ch 813 the rows she says
   out loud in the dark come back on boards in two hands. The second is fair, even, a little smaller than hers,
   with not one place where the nib had to be lifted, and **under every figure of hers it is the same figure one
   day further on.** She has read twenty boards; on nineteen the second row differs from hers and on one it does
   not, and that one is the board out of twenty where she cannot tell a correction from a copy. **The second row
   begins four figures back from the head of each board and runs to the end, and that is how she knows it is
   new.** She can check fourteen of the twenty against her own mouth and **it is right on all fourteen**; she
   cannot put her mouth against the other six. **She has not asked who is doing it and has said out loud that she
   is not going to, and she is not going to tell it to stop, and she wrote nothing against it.** **She has
   decided, and said so out loud, that she is not going to work out whether the fair hand and the third hand are
   the same hand, and that the decision is a decision and not a giving up.** Open, and Batch 0004 may not settle it
   before the refusal goes down the stair.
2. **HIS YES, AT ch 812 — new in this batch, and she does not know it happened.** One sentence, present tense,
   away from her hearing, to a piece of wood about the length of his own forearm: **I will keep what she says out
   loud in this room.** **He did not get into the place, did not sit in it and did not put his weight on it**, and
   all three are named in the chapter as decisions he made on purpose. **No card in ch 811–825 tells her, no card
   puts her and that sentence in the same room, and she says out loud at ch 822 that it is not said to him.**
3. **THE PLACE DESCRIBED AS KEPT — advanced, and it is described, not offered.** A board came up with the
   fifteenth bundle at the second hour on the first of the month beginning 2,714 and says where the place is, how
   long it is, how wide it is, that it is not a seat, that nobody has sat in it, that it has been kept for four
   hundred years, **that it is not given to anybody, and that it is kept by being there when the figures are said
   out loud.** **There is no job in it and no second half of it and no terms and no price and no binding**, and
   she says all of that out loud at about the fourth hour. Open, and the terms are ch 826's.
4. **THE NO — new in this batch, and it cannot be unsaid.** Said out loud, standing, in the ordinary voice, at
   about the ninth hour and ten minutes on the fifth of the month beginning 2,714, into twenty-two paces of dark:
   **a no to a person keeping what she says out loud in this room.** It is not a no to the record, not a no to the
   place, not a no to the boy and not a no to the fair hand, and she says each of those out loud so that the no
   is a no to the keeping and not to the record. **Nothing was written on anything and nothing went down that
   stair.** Open, and it goes on a blank leaf at ch 838.
5. **THE FIVE BLANK LEAVES — advanced, and none of them is written on.** Eight leaves against her fourth rib for
   nineteen years; three written on, the last when she was nine and it was a date; five blank and blank since she
   was nine. **She has said out loud, when and how and on what morning**: the first morning anything comes up that
   stair, the leaf in her hand at about the first hour, the sentence said out loud before the nib goes on the
   paper, and then the leaf folded and put in the hand that goes down the stair. **She has also said out loud that
   she is not going to write on one tonight, or tomorrow, or on any morning in between**, because a leaf is for the
   sentence and not for the wanting to have said it. Open, and Batch 0004 owns the writing and the sending.
6. **THE GAP IN THE WEAR — new in this batch, and told to nobody.** At ch 820, looking along the edge of the worn
   place instead of down onto it, she finds **a place about two fingers wide in the middle of the length of the
   wear where the wood is not worn down at all, and it is not worn on either side of the gap either, so the gap
   does not have edges to it and there is no way at all of looking at that which makes it an edge.** The other two
   things about the wear were said out loud in this room at ch 805 and she has said she will not say them again.
   **She has told nobody this one and has said out loud that she is not going to, and she has not put her hand in
   the place, or her weight, and nobody has put her in it.** Open, and Batch 0004 may not have her say it aloud.
7. **THE BOY OF SIXTEEN — advanced, and still not bound, not entered and not sent.** She has said all three out
   loud in this batch with the reason attached under each one, and has told him about the board with his name on
   it before he has to find it out, **because there is one exception and she made it and did not make it and cannot
   stop it.** He asked for the second hour of a first of a month at ch 821 and she said yes in about a second;
   she had refused him the same thing at ch 817 and **has said out loud that she changed her mind in three days.**
   He works the oil count the way she works days and the two counts have never agreed, because oil is not days. He
   is not on a page of any book, nothing above the line knows he exists, and he comes up eight hundred and eighty
   steps on his own account.
8. **THE BUNDLE UNDER THE FAR END — untouched in this batch.** Two turns and not a knot, corded round twice,
   touched by two pairs of hands and not opened, five times in nineteen years and never once in light. Open, and
   Batch 0004 may not open it before the refusal goes down the stair.
9. **THE BEARER — advanced.** **At ch 818 the fifteenth bundle came up in the dark and put forty boards on that
   table and went back down and not one word of it was said, and then a single stroke, the second time in thirty
   days, from the same place as the forty that went before it, lasting about a count of nine.** Open.
10. **THE DAY SHE FOUND THE PLACE — still open, three anchors still disagree.** **No chapter in ch 811–825 stated a
    day for the finding, did the subtraction, or treated any one of the three as settled.**
11. **WHAT THE LAMP GOING OUT MEANS — untouched.** The cage lamp is out for a hundred and eighty-seven days at ch
    825 and nothing in this batch relit it, fed it, or discussed it as relightable.

**NEW AND IRREVERSIBLE AT ch 822, CLOSED AND NOT TO BE RE-OPENED.** **She said no out loud, standing, in the
ordinary voice, in the dark, to a thing that works** — the correction that is right on all fourteen of the
mornings she can check, the lamp that comes up eight hundred and eighty steps on a boy's back, the fair hand, the
board with a name on it and the board that describes a place, every part of it working. **She said out loud what
it is not a no to, because a no said there cannot be unsaid and because she refuses the keeping and not the
record, and a refusal that read as a refusal of the record would be a different thing with a different price.**
**She did not move the strip of pale wood off the top of that table and she did not vacate the record and she said
both of those out loud so that the no could not be taken for either.** Nothing was written on anything. Nothing
went down that stair.

**Not to be named in Batch 0004 before its own chapter:** the moral answer · the Anchor · the tax · what the
walls are for · the bargain's mercy · **anything that speaks to him that is not her** · **the terms or the price
before ch 826** · **the speech of the one at that table before ch 826** · **the acceptance before ch 838** · any
second look · any second occasion for the one look spent at ch 776 · the writing on a leaf before ch 838 · **the
gap in the wear said out loud to anybody**.

**New question for Volume 15, carried verbatim from `outline/volume-14.md`:** he is readable now by the thing that made him, and he said yes once where she could not hear it, and she said no twice where everything could hear it. The refusal went down the stair in her own hand and nothing came back up. **The question is not whether it heard. It is what a thing that reads does with a no.**

### What Volume 15 opens, and what a card must not assume has been settled

1. **THE BUILDING HAS MORE ABOVE HER ROOM.** Nine landings and a stair that does not stop at nine. **The first
   time in fifteen volumes anything has been above her.**
2. **HER ROOM IS ONE OF HUNDREDS**, and every one of them has a kept place at the far side and nobody in it.
3. **THE SCRIBE SPEAKS** — at ch 863, in the grammar of an announcement, one sentence at a time, and she hears
   both sides. **`outline/series.md` §7 milestone 8, spent once.**
4. **HE IS ANNOUNCED** at ch 866, by choice, without asking her. **Cost: he can be anywhere but inside her, and
   she can hear his half.**
5. **THE MORAL ANSWER** — the Anchor, the ceiling, the tax, nine hundred years — at ch 878 and ch 885, once, worked
   out of her own arithmetic, and **his share is the small part of it.**
6. **A NUMBER IN THE RECORD THAT CANNOT BE TAKEN OUT**, said out loud standing in the ordinary voice at ch 885.
7. **THE PEAK** at ch 894 and **THE SISTERHOOD'S FIRST ENTRY**, with the boy, said out loud first.
8. **THE BARGAIN**, on the table at ch 900, good in every part, unanswered.
9. **THE QUESTION SHE HAS NEVER ASKED HIM** — what he wants — asked at ch 900, unanswered.

### What Batch 0001 must not walk past

The six terms are said one at a time and the second one is held alone for a hundred and forty counts and means
the stair, **and that has been said and is not said again in that room**. The strip of pale wood went back under
the table on the seventh of this month and why. **She cannot be alone in the room any more — named once at ch 828
and not to be named again.** The two refusals and the readability do not cancel and both stand. The bar and its
four hundred and thirty-six days. The first thing she ever sent cannot be called back and she would not call it
back. **A recap is not a dramatic restaging; if a card wants one of these, the card is wrong, not the page.**

**And, for Batch 0001 specifically:** no stage · no binding and no name for the boy · no Scribe and the word
`scribe` 0 · no moral answer, not even hinted · **one bracketed line in fifteen and it is the bundle at 2,804** ·
no step count for the stair above the room and no oil figure of any kind · **no card may say the first of a month
has happened before ch 850 or after ch 849** — that is the Volume 14 Batch 0004 defect this run has already paid
for once.

### What Batch 0001 did to the batch, in one paragraph

**She stopped keeping a record in a room, asked for a lamp out loud, climbed a stair she refused to count, found that
the room she had been standing in for five hundred and twenty-four days is one of a great many with a kept place at the
far side of it, found that the refusal she had lived behind for a month was not hers, missed a first of a month on
purpose from nine landings up, went past the ninth landing, and walked out of the only room she has had since she was
nine years old with a lamp in her left hand and did not come back down.**

### What Batch 0002 did to the batch, in one paragraph

**She left a lamp burning on the third landing and climbed in the dark, asked a bearer his name and learned what four hundred years cost him, named two firsts she did not go down for, found a doorless landing and sentences starting above it, heard somebody speak to him through her and gave it its word, failed to refuse for him, lost the last private room either of them had by his choice, heard fifteen years of asking in one morning, found one useful thing in it, heard her own room run with nobody in it, and said out loud where it could be heard that she wants it for him.**

### Four things Batch 0003 must not inherit as clean prose

1. **That the Scribe is answered, explained, or faced.** It reports, never advises; she named it and it did not object, and that is all.
2. **That he is forgiven, thanked, or withdrawn from.** None of those happened; she stays, unwanting back what she said.
3. **That the tax, the Anchor, the walls, or the bargain have been stated.** None is on the page; Batch 0003 opens all of that.
4. **That hearing his half is free, or that the room needs her.** The hearing is paid for and cannot be un-paid; the room runs on firsts with nobody in it.

### Four things Batch 0002 must not inherit as clean prose

1. **That the light is nearly out, or any figure of how long it lasts.** There is none on the page and none may be
   invented. His count is his and hers is hers and neither is converted into the other.
2. **That the stair above the room has a step count, a height, a top, or a number of rooms in it.** It has none of
   those on the page. She counts landings; the landings are nine and then it keeps going.
3. **That the cells are explained.** They are not. A table, forty boards with a second number on the top one, a bundle,
   a lamp out, and a kept place that nobody has ever sat in. Nothing says what any of it is for, and the chapter that
   would say it is ch 878.
4. **That she is coming back down.** She said out loud at ch 855 that she was never going to stand four feet off the
   near end of that table again, and she took a lamp and her own arithmetic and nothing else, and the strip of pale
   wood is still under that table.
